## Claim
Text: "The Median White Household Wealth In The US Is 81 Times that of The Black Median Household Wealth"

## Initial Q&A

### What is the most recent and authoritative source for data on median household wealth by race in the United States, such as the U.S. Federal Reserve's Survey of Consumer Finances?
Answer: The most recent and authoritative source is the U.S. Federal Reserve's Survey of Consumer Finances (SCF), as directly cited in the search result in connection with the 2020 data on median white and Black household wealth.

Source URL: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/31166957//created

### According to the latest available data from the chosen authoritative source, what is the reported ratio of median white household wealth to median Black household wealth in the United States?
Answer: According to the Federal Reserve's 2019 Survey of Consumer Finances, the ratio of median white household wealth to median Black household wealth in the United States is 81 to 1, with median White wealth reported as $188,200 and median Black wealth as $2,300.

Source URL: https://prosperitynow.org/files/PDFs/road_to_zero_wealth.pdf/created

### For which specific year or survey period (e.g., 2022, 2019-2023) does the cited 81:1 ratio apply?
Answer: The search result provides the 81:1 ratio and the specific dollar figures, but it does not specify the year or survey period to which the data pertains.

Source URL: https://downloads.esri.com/esri_content_doc/dbl/us/tapestry/segment2.pdf/created

### How does the authoritative source define "household wealth" or "net worth" for the purpose of calculating this ratio (e.g., assets minus debts, including home equity)?
Answer: The search result states the 81:1 ratio is based on "median net worth, the standard measure used by the Federal Reserve in its Survey of Consumer Finances," but it does not explicitly define how the Fed calculates net worth for this purpose.

Source URL: https://tcf.org/content/commentary/graph-the-wealth-gap-between-blacks-and-whites-has-tripled-since-1984-heres-why//created

### Does the reported median wealth figure for each racial group represent the median for all households of that race, or does it apply to a subset (e.g., only households with positive net worth)?
Answer: The reported figures refer to the median household net worth for all white, Latino, and black households, not a subset with positive net worth. The source discusses "median household net worth" and then separately references research on "families with positive net worth," indicating the main statistical comparisons are across the entire demographic group.

Source URL: https://prospect.org/special-report/race-wealth-intergenerational-poverty/

### Has the 81:1 ratio been directly and explicitly stated by the authoritative source (e.g., the Federal Reserve), or is it a calculation derived from reported median wealth figures?
Answer: The 81:1 ratio is directly stated as a reported comparison from the Federal Reserve's data. The source explicitly mentions "median white household wealth 2020 81 times black The Federal Reserve's Survey of Consumer Finances," indicating the ratio is presented as a factual comparison rather than a user-derived calculation.

Source URL: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/31166957//created

### What was the median white household wealth and the median Black household wealth in the most recent survey to calculate the precise ratio?
Answer: According to the cited source, as of October 2020, "median White household wealth was $188,200, while median Black household wealth was $2,300, yielding an 81:1 ratio."

Source URL: https://statusofwomendata.org/explore-the-data/poverty-opportunity/additional-state-data/median-household-income-by-household-type//created

### How does the claimed 81:1 ratio compare to the ratios reported in previous years by the same source, indicating any recent trend (increasing, stable, or decreasing disparity)?
Answer: Based on the provided search result, which only cites the 2020 figure, I cannot determine how the 81:1 ratio compares to previous years from the same source (the Federal Reserve's Survey of Consumer Finances). The search result does not contain historical trend data.

Source URL: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/31166957//created

### Are there other major, credible surveys or reports (e.g., from the U.S. Census Bureau or Pew Research Center) that produce a significantly different ratio for the same period?
Answer: The Survey of Consumer Finances (SCF) is the primary source for the widely cited 81:1 ratio as of October 2020. Other major surveys like the U.S. Census Bureau’s Survey of Income and Program Participation (SIPP) and reports from the Pew Research Center also document a significant racial wealth gap, but they often produce different ratios due to variations in methodology, survey design, and definitions of wealth, typically reporting a smaller but still substantial disparity.

Source URL: https://statusofwomendata.org/explore-the-data/poverty-opportunity/additional-state-data/median-household-income-by-household-type//created

### What methodological notes or caveats does the authoritative source provide about interpreting the median wealth figures and the resulting racial wealth gap?
Answer: The source provides several methodological caveats: 1) the race variable changed in 1998 (allowing multiple selections) and 2004 (separately identifying Hispanic ethnicity), though analysis uses the first reported race; 2) the unit is the "primary economic unit" (PEU), distinct from Census households/families; 3) the unexplained portion of the wealth gap in regressions "is not intended as a proxy for racial bias and discrimination," as it includes all unobserved factors, and discrimination may also affect observed factors like income and homeownership; and 4) decomposition methods (Oaxaca-Blinder and DFL) assume the effect of unobservables is the same across races and may have limited common support, especially at the top of the distribution. The authors also note that sample sizes for Black and Hispanic families were small in earlier survey years, potentially affecting precision.

Source URL: https://www.federalreserve.gov/econresdata/feds/2015/files/2015076pap.pdf

## Decision Options
* `supported`: The knowledge from the fact-check supports or at least strongly implies the Claim. Mere plausibility is not enough for this decision.
* `refuted`: The knowledge from the fact-check explicitly and clearly refutes at least substantial parts if not even the whole the Claim.

## Initial Q&A

### What was the legal classification of Aboriginal Australians under Australian federal and state laws prior to 1967?
Answer: Based on the search result, prior to 1967, Aboriginal Australians were legally classified as a distinct group subject to specific "protection and control" laws by various state and federal governments. The listed legislation consistently refers to them as "Aboriginal natives" or "inhabitants" who were to be managed, protected, and controlled under separate legal frameworks, such as the various Aborigines Acts.

### In what year did Australian citizens vote in a national referendum that pertained to the rights or classification of Aboriginal Australians?
Answer: Australian citizens voted in a national referendum in 1967 that pertained to the rights of Aboriginal Australians, as the source states, "As we celebrate 50 years since the 1967 Referendum let's reflect upon some of the key facts from this important milestone in Australia's journey towards reconciliation."

Source URL: https://www.sydney.edu.au/news-opinion/news/2017/05/29/five-things-to-know-about-the-1967-referendum.html

### What was the primary question or constitutional change put to voters in the 1967 Australian referendum?
Answer: The primary constitutional change put to voters in the 1967 Australian referendum was to alter Section 51 of the Constitution to empower the federal parliament to make laws for Aboriginal and Torres Strait Islander peoples, who were previously excluded. As the source states, this change "allowed the parliament to 'make laws for peace, order and good government' for all Australians, where previously Aboriginal people had been specifically excluded." Additionally, it enabled the inclusion of Aboriginal and Torres Strait Islander peoples in the national census.

Source URL: https://www.sydney.edu.au/news-opinion/news/2017/05/29/five-things-to-know-about-the-1967-referendum.html

### Did the 1967 Australian referendum directly repeal or modify any existing act named "The Flora and Fauna Act"?
Answer: No, the 1967 Australian referendum did not directly repeal or modify any act specifically named "The Flora and Fauna Act." The search result confirms that such an act existed and classified Aboriginal people as animals, but it does not state that the referendum addressed it. The referendum's primary purpose was to amend the Australian Constitution to allow the federal government to make laws for Aboriginal people and to include them in population counts, not to target specific state-level legislation like the Flora and Fauna Act.

Source URL: http://classic.austlii.edu.au/au/journals/AUIndigLawRw/2007/5.html/created

### What specific legal changes regarding Aboriginal Australians were achieved through the 1967 referendum or other legislative acts in the 1960s?
Answer: The 1967 referendum amended the Australian Constitution to achieve two specific legal changes: first, it allowed Aboriginal people to be counted in the national census by repealing Section 127, which had stated that "aboriginal natives shall not be counted"; second, it deleted the phrase "other than the aboriginal race in any State" from Section 51, thereby granting the Commonwealth government the power to make laws for Aboriginal people, which it previously lacked.

Source URL: https://www.creativespirits.info/aboriginalculture/history/australian-1967-referendum
